10	        "text": "I have all the information needed. Here is the report.\n\n---\n\n# FUSE IPNS First-Publish Embedded-Sequence Mechanics\n\n## 1. What the \"embedded sequence\" is\n\nThe embedded sequence is a field **inside the signed IPNS record bytes** — specifically the `\"Sequence\"` key in the CBOR `data` map, which is covered by the V2 signature.\n\nThe record is built in `crates/core/src/ipns.rs`. `build_cbor_data` (`ipns.rs:127`) places the sequence into the CBOR map:\n\n```rust\n// crates/core/src/ipns.rs:142-145\n(\n    CborValue::Text(\"Sequence\".to_string()),\n    CborValue::Integer(sequence.into()),\n),\n```\n\n`create_ipns_record` (`ipns.rs:234`) builds that CBOR and then signs **over the CBOR bytes**:\n\n```rust\n// crates/core/src/ipns.rs:249-251\nlet cbor_data = build_cbor_data(value, &validity, sequence_number, ttl)?;\nlet signature_v2 = compute_v2_signature(ed25519_private_key, &cbor_data)?;\n```\n\nThe V2 signature is over `\"ipns-signature:\" || cbor_data` (`compute_v2_signature`, `ipns.rs:220-229`), so the sequence is inside the signed payload. The sequence is **also** duplicated as a plaintext protobuf field 5 in the marshaled wire bytes (`marshal_ipns_record`, `ipns.rs:287`: `encode_proto_varint(&mut buf, 5, record.sequence)`), but the authoritative, signature-covered copy is the CBOR one. Verify reads only the CBOR copy (see §2).\n\n`create_ipns_record`'s third positional argument is exactly this embedded sequence:\n\n```rust\n// crates/core/src/ipns.rs:234-239\npub fn create_ipns_record(\n    ed25519_private_key: &[u8; 32],\n    value: &str,\n    sequence_number: u64,   // <- the embedded sequence\n    lifetime_ms: u64,\n) -> Result<IpnsRecord, IpnsError>\n```\n\n## 2. What `resp_seq` is and where it comes from\n\n`resp_seq` is the **DB-authoritative** sequence returned by the API resolve endpoint, NOT the record's own embedded value.\n\n- The api-client struct: `crates/api-client/src/types.rs:130-143` — `IpnsResolveResponse.sequence_number: String` (\"Current sequence number as a string (bigint from backend)\").\n- `resolve_ipns` (`crates/api-client/src/ipns.rs:14-51`) GETs `/ipns/resolve?ipnsName=…` and deserializes that field straight from the backend JSON.\n- In `verify.rs`, it's parsed to `u64`:\n\n```rust\n// crates/fuse/src/verify.rs:120-123\nlet resp_seq = resp\n    .sequence_number\n    .parse::<u64>()\n    .map_err(|e| VerifyError::Invalid(format!(\"parse response sequence_number: {}\", e)))?;\n```\n\nBy contrast, `embedded_seq` comes from decoding the signed CBOR `data` blob via `decode_ipns_cbor_data` (`crates/core/src/ipns.rs:81`), which reads the `\"Sequence\"` CBOR key (`ipns.rs:104-113`):\n\n```rust\n// crates/fuse/src/verify.rs:87-89\nlet (embedded_value, embedded_seq) =\n    cipherbox_core::ipns::decode_ipns_cbor_data(&data_bytes)\n        .map_err(|e| VerifyError::Invalid(format!(\"CBOR decode failed: {}\", e)))?;\n```\n\nSo: `embedded_seq` = signed-CBOR `\"Sequence\"` from the record itself; `resp_seq` = the backend's DB sequence counter.\n\n## 3. The verify.rs comparison code (verbatim) and when the skew fires\n\n```rust\n// crates/fuse/src/verify.rs:124-130\nlet seq_ok = embedded_seq == resp_seq || (resp_seq == 1 && embedded_seq == 0);\nif !seq_ok {\n    return Err(VerifyError::Invalid(format!(\n        \"IPNS sequence binding mismatch: embedded={}, response seq={}\",\n        embedded_seq, resp_seq\n    )));\n}\n```\n\nThe allowance fires in **exactly one case**: a freshly-created folder's first-generation record whose record carries `embedded_seq == 0` while the backend DB reports `resp_seq == 1`. Strict equality is required for every other sequence (e.g. `embedded=0, resp=2` is rejected — confirmed by the unit test `bind_verified_seq_skew_only_applies_to_first_publish`, `verify.rs:256-265`).\n\nOn the skew path the function returns the **DB-authoritative** `resp_seq` (1), not the embedded 0, so downstream forward math (`next publish = seq + 1`) computes 2 (`verify.rs:138-145`; pinned by test `bind_verified_first_publish_seq_skew_returns_ok`, `verify.rs:244-253`). The inline comment (`verify.rs:100-119`) explicitly ties this to the API's D-09 first-publish gate and notes tightening to strict `embedded_seq == resp_seq` is deferred to Phase 60 pending publish-site unification + a republish migration.\n\n## 4. Which publish sites embed 0 vs 1 (post-Phase-59)\n\nFolder first-publish sites (the relevant ones for embedded-sequence-0 records):\n\n**Embed 0 (interactive live folder-create — NOT yet unified):**\n\n- `crates/fuse/src/write_ops/implementation/mkdir.rs:173-175` (Unix/macOS mkdir):\n  ```rust\n  let record = cipherbox_core::ipns::create_ipns_record(\n      &ipns_key_arr, &value, 0, 86_400_000,\n  )\n  ```\n  Published with `expected_sequence_number: None` and `coordinator.record_publish(&ipns_name_clone, 0)` (`mkdir.rs:190, 194`).\n\n- `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:201-203` (Windows mkdir):\n  ```rust\n  let record = cipherbox_core::ipns::create_ipns_record(\n      &ipns_key_arr, &value, 0, 86_400_000,\n  )\n  ```\n  Same `expected_sequence_number: None` / `record_publish(..., 0)` (`write_ops.rs:216, 220`).\n\n**Embed 1 (crash-recovery replay first-publish — unified with TS SDK per Phase 59 Finding F):**\n\n- `crates/fuse/src/replay.rs:628` in `publish_child_folder_metadata`:\n  ```rust\n  let record = cipherbox_core::ipns::create_ipns_record(&ipns_key_arr, &value, 1, 86_400_000)\n  ```\n  with `coordinator.record_publish(child_ipns_name, 1)` (`replay.rs:668`). The doc comment at `replay.rs:592-601` and the inline note at `replay.rs:659-661` confirm the live mkdir path \"still embeds 0\" while replay embeds 1; both are accepted as a first publish, full unification deferred to Phase 60.\n\nThe other `create_ipns_record` call sites (`mkdir.rs:220`, `windows/write_ops.rs:238`, `replay.rs:542`, `content_ops.rs:169/211`, `metadata.rs:261/371/542`) are **non-first** publishes that embed `new_seq = seq + 1`, not a literal 0 or 1, so they are outside the first-publish convention.\n\nNet post-Phase-59: the two interactive mkdir paths embed **0**; the replay child-folder path embeds **1**.\n\n## 5. CRITICAL: changing an existing published record's embedded sequence from 0 to 1\n\n**It requires RE-SIGNING the record, which requires the IPNS private key.** The embedded sequence cannot be changed without re-signing.\n\nReason, citing the signature coverage:\n\n- The embedded sequence lives inside the CBOR `data` blob (`build_cbor_data`, `crates/core/src/ipns.rs:142-145`).\n- The V2 signature is computed over `\"ipns-signature:\" || cbor_data` (`compute_v2_signature`, `ipns.rs:220-229`; produced in `create_ipns_record` at `ipns.rs:249-251`). Changing the `\"Sequence\"` value mutates `cbor_data`, which invalidates `signature_v2`.\n- The verify path validates that exact binding: `verify_ipns_resolve_signature` (`crates/api-client/src/ipns.rs:104-111`) rebuilds `\"ipns-signature:\" || cbor_data` and runs `verify_ed25519(&signed_data, &sig, &pub_key)`; a record with a flipped sequence but the old signature returns `Some(false)` → `VerifyError::Invalid`. And `verify.rs:87-89` reads the sequence **from that same signed CBOR blob**, so you cannot bump the embedded value while keeping a valid signature.\n\nTo produce a record with `embedded=1`, you must call `create_ipns_record(&ed25519_private_key, value, 1, …)` — i.e. hold the Ed25519 IPNS private key, recompute `cbor_data` with `Sequence=1`, and recompute both `signature_v1` and `signature_v2` (`ipns.rs:249-253`). The plaintext protobuf field-5 copy (`marshal_ipns_record`, `ipns.rs:287`) could in principle be edited byte-wise without the key, but that is not the value verify trusts — verify reads only the signature-covered CBOR copy — so editing it alone does not change what verify sees and would just create a record whose plaintext and signed sequences disagree.\n\nMechanically: a \"live record migration\" of embedded-0 records to embedded-1 is a **re-sign + republish** operation gated on private-key access. In FUSE that key is the `ipns_private_key` (zeroized `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>`) used at the publish sites (`mkdir.rs:170-171`, `replay.rs:623-626`); for TEE-enrolled records the key also exists wrapped under the TEE public key (`replay.rs:635-643`), so re-signing would need either the client-held private key or the TEE that can decrypt+sign.\n\n### Key file:line references\n\n- `crates/fuse/src/verify.rs:120-145` — skew comparison + DB-authoritative return\n- `crates/fuse/src/publish.rs:13-27` — `next_file_publish_sequence` (first publish → 1)\n- `crates/core/src/ipns.rs:81-121` — `decode_ipns_cbor_data` (extracts embedded seq)\n- `crates/core/src/ipns.rs:127-159` — `build_cbor_data` (`\"Sequence\"` in signed CBOR)\n- `crates/core/src/ipns.rs:220-266` — `compute_v2_signature` + `create_ipns_record` (sig covers CBOR)\n- `crates/api-client/src/ipns.rs:104-124` — `verify_ipns_resolve_signature` (rebuilds + verifies signed bytes)\n- `crates/api-client/src/types.rs:130-143` — `IpnsResolveResponse.sequence_number` (source of `resp_seq`)\n- `crates/fuse/src/write_ops/implementation/mkdir.rs:173` and `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:201` — embed 0\n- `crates/fuse/src/replay.rs:628` — embed 1"

14	        "text": "I have all the information needed. The TEE worker computes `newSequenceNumber` (the API only sends the current `sequenceNumber` and receives back the new one + signed record). Here is the report.\n\n# IPNS Publish/Resolve/Verify Path — Server-Side Map\n\n## 1. Does the API sign records? No — it relays client-signed (and TEE-signed) records.\n\nThe API never signs. The publish endpoint description states it explicitly, and the service only *verifies* a pre-signed record before relaying.\n\n`apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.controller.ts:46-49` (publish endpoint doc):\n> \"Relay a pre-signed IPNS record to the IPFS network via delegated routing. The client signs the record locally; backend relays to the delegated routing service and tracks the folder for TEE republishing.\"\n\n`apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts:40-42` (`publishRecord`):\n> \"Publish a pre-signed IPNS record to the IPFS network via delegated routing and track the folder in the database for TEE republishing\"\n\nThe only other signing actor is the **TEE worker** (out of process, hardware enclave). The API sends a batch to the TEE for signing and gets back a signed record — see §4/§5.\n\n## 2. IPNS record table columns — stores the FULL signed record bytes\n\nTable: `folder_ipns`. Entity at `apps/api/src/ipns/entities/folder-ipns.entity.ts`. Columns:\n\n| Column (DB) | Type | Field | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `id` | uuid PK | `id` | |\n| `user_id` | uuid (indexed, FK→users) | `userId` | denormalized creator marker only (entity:14-19) |\n| `ipns_name` | varchar(255), **UNIQUE** | `ipnsName` | canonical key — one row per name (`@Unique(['ipnsName'])`, entity:19) |\n| `latest_cid` | varchar(255) nullable | `latestCid` | authoritative CID returned on resolve |\n| `sequence_number` | **bigint** default 0 | `sequenceNumber` | returned as string; DB-authoritative seq (entity:49-50) |\n| `signed_record` | **bytea** nullable | `signedRecord` | **the full marshalled signed IPNS record bytes** — \"the thing whose embedded sequence is fixed at sign time\" (entity:53-57) |\n| `public_key` | bytea nullable | `publicKey` | raw 32-byte Ed25519 pubkey (entity:59-64) |\n| `encrypted_ipns_private_key` | bytea nullable | `encryptedIpnsPrivateKey` | ECIES-wrapped, TEE-only (entity:66-72) |\n| `key_epoch` | int nullable | `keyEpoch` | TEE epoch (entity:74-80) |\n| `is_root` | boolean default false | `isRoot` | |\n| `record_type` | varchar(10) default 'folder' | `recordType` | 'folder' \\| 'file' |\n| `created_at` / `updated_at` | timestamp | | |\n\nSo it stores **both** the full signed record bytes (`signed_record`, where the embedded sequence is fixed) **and** metadata columns (`latest_cid`, `sequence_number`, `public_key`). It does NOT store a separate `signature` or `validity` column — those live inside the `signed_record` protobuf.\n\nKey fact for migration: `sequence_number` (DB) and the embedded sequence inside `signed_record` can diverge. Per `parseCachedRecord` (`apps/api/src/ipns/ipns-record.codec.ts:65-75`), the DB columns are treated as authoritative because the record bytes may contain a pre-increment value (e.g. embedded sequence 0 on first publish while DB row = '1'). This is the embedded-sequence-0 condition the migration targets.\n\n## 3. Signature verification chokepoints\n\n**On publish** — `apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts:87-89`:\n```ts\nif (!(await verifyIpnsRecordSignature(dto.ipnsName, recordBytes))) {\n  throw new BadRequestException('IPNS record signature verification failed');\n}\n```\nThis is the PR #544 Ed25519 chokepoint. Implementation in `packages/crypto/src/ipns/verify-record.ts:33-53`: derives the Ed25519 pubkey from the `k51...` name (CIDv1/libp2p-key identity multihash) and calls `validate(peerId.publicKey, marshalledRecord)` from `ipns/validator`. The `validate()` call also rejects expired-EOL and V1-only records (verify-record.ts:26-27, 47-48). Authority = key possession (any holder of a validly-signed record may update the name; the cache is keyed by `ipnsName`, not user — service:84-86, 211-214).\n\n**On resolve** — there is **no signature verification**. `resolveRecord` (`ipns.service.ts:444-551`) only *parses* records (`parseIpnsRecordBytes`/`parseCachedRecord`) and picks the higher sequence between network and DB cache (lines 498-523). It returns `signatureV2`/`data`/`pubKey` so the **client** can verify. The controller bundles those all-or-nothing for client-side verification (`ipns.controller.ts:202-213`).\n\n## 4. Does the API have the IPNS private key? No — only the ECIES-encrypted form; the TEE decrypts.\n\nThe API stores only the **encrypted** private key, never plaintext:\n- `folder_ipns.encrypted_ipns_private_key` — \"ECIES-wrapped Ed25519 private key for TEE republishing / Encrypted with TEE public key, only decryptable by TEE\" (`folder-ipns.entity.ts:66-72`).\n- `ipns_republish_schedule.encrypted_ipns_key` (bytea, NOT NULL) — \"TEE-encrypted Ed25519 private key for IPNS signing. Only decryptable by the TEE worker.\" (`republish-schedule.entity.ts:36-40`). The schedule entity also notes `last_error` \"NEVER contains key material\" (lines 90-95).\n\nIn `upsertFolderIpns`, the API only ever stores the hex-decoded *encrypted* key (`existing.encryptedIpnsPrivateKey = Buffer.from(encryptedIpnsPrivateKey, 'hex')`, service:327, 365-367) and forwards it to the republish enrollment. **The API cannot re-sign** — it has no plaintext key. Re-signing happens only in the TEE worker (`tee.service.ts:107-108`: \"The TEE worker decrypts IPNS keys, signs new records, and returns results\").\n\n## 5. Existing republish / re-sign path the migration could reuse\n\nYes — the **TEE republish path**, but it is a scheduled background job, not a callable HTTP endpoint:\n\n- `RepublishService.republishDue()` batches due `ipns_republish_schedule` entries and calls `teeService.republish(teeEntries)` (`republish.service.ts:109`).\n- The TEE returns `{ success, signedRecord, newSequenceNumber, upgradedEncryptedKey?, upgradedKeyEpoch? }` (`tee.service.ts:30-40`). The API does **not** compute the new sequence — the TEE does (`newSequenceNumber`, sent back to the API).\n- On success the API publishes via `publishSignedRecord(ipnsName, signedRecord)` (`republish.service.ts:136`, impl at :201-202 — base64-decodes and pushes to delegated routing) and writes the new bytes back via `syncFolderIpnsSequence` → `folderIpnsRepository.update({ userId, ipnsName }, { sequenceNumber, signedRecord })` (`republish.service.ts:372-391`).\n\nThere is **no** API HTTP endpoint that re-signs or republishes on demand. The IPNS controller exposes only `POST /ipns/publish`, `POST /ipns/publish-batch`, `POST /ipns/unenroll`, `GET /ipns/resolve` (`ipns.controller.ts:43,85,126,157`). `republish-health.controller.ts` exposes only `GET /republish-health`. So any migration that needs fresh signatures must either go through the TEE batch path (enrolled entries only, requires the encrypted key + epoch present) or accept new client-signed records via `POST /ipns/publish`.\n\n## 6. How `resp_seq` (returned sequence) is determined\n\nThe returned `sequenceNumber` is the **DB column**, not the embedded record sequence.\n\n`publishRecord` returns `folder.sequenceNumber` (the saved DB row), not the parsed embedded value (`ipns.service.ts:132-136`):\n```ts\nreturn { success: true, ipnsName: dto.ipnsName, sequenceNumber: folder.sequenceNumber };\n```\n\nHow the DB `sequence_number` is set in `upsertFolderIpns`:\n- **New row:** hard-coded to `'1'` to match the client's first-publish record (client computes `0n + 1n = 1n`), even though the embedded record sequence may be `0` (`ipns.service.ts:356-358`).\n- **Existing row, normal forward publish:** `existing.sequenceNumber = (BigInt(existing.sequenceNumber) + 1n).toString()` (`ipns.service.ts:314-316`).\n- **Idempotent republish** (embedded == DB seq, the TEE re-sign case): DB seq is **not** incremented, but `latestCid`/`signedRecord` are still updated (`ipns.service.ts:288-291`, 314).\n\nEmbedded-sequence gating (the seq carried inside the signed bytes) is validated against the DB seq before this: first publish must be embedded ∈ {0,1} (lines 281-285); existing must be `dbSeq` (idempotent) or `dbSeq + 1` (forward), else 400 rollback/jump (lines 286-303). The optional `expectedSequenceNumber` CAS (lines 245-256) compares the client's expectation against the DB column and 409s on mismatch.\n\n**On resolve**, `resp_seq` is the max of network vs DB-cached sequence (`ipns.service.ts:498-523`); the DB cache wins when its sequence is higher (lines 502-510), and equal-sequence network results are enriched with cached signature fields (lines 512-519).\n\n---\n\n### Migration-relevant summary (no solution proposed)\n- The \"embedded sequence 0\" lives inside `folder_ipns.signed_record` (the marshalled, signature-covered bytes). Fixing it requires producing a **new signature** over a record with a corrected embedded sequence.\n- The API holds **no plaintext key** and **cannot re-sign**; only the TEE (with `encrypted_ipns_private_key`/`encrypted_ipns_key` + matching `key_epoch`) or the client can produce a new signed record.\n- The DB `sequence_number` column is already the authoritative resp_seq and can diverge from the embedded value; resolve/publish both treat the column as authoritative while still relaying/storing the (stale-embedded) bytes.\n- No on-demand re-sign HTTP endpoint exists; reusable signing flows are (a) the scheduled TEE batch republish (`RepublishService.republishDue` → `teeService.republish` → `publishSignedRecord` → `syncFolderIpnsSequence`) limited to enrolled rows, or (b) accepting client-signed records via `POST /ipns/publish`.\n\nKey files: `apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts`, `apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.controller.ts`, `apps/api/src/ipns/ipns-record.codec.ts`, `apps/api/src/ipns/entities/folder-ipns.entity.ts`, `packages/crypto/src/ipns/verify-record.ts`, `apps/api/src/republish/republish.service.ts`, `apps/api/src/republish/republish-schedule.entity.ts`, `apps/api/src/tee/tee.service.ts`, and migrations under `apps/api/src/migrations/` (`1700000000000-FullSchema.ts`, `1740500000000-BackfillIpnsSequenceNumbers.ts`, `1743200000000-AddSignedRecordToFolderIpns.ts`, `1743300000000-AddPublicKeyToFolderIpns.ts`, `1749300000000-IpnsCacheKeyedByName.ts`)."

18	        "text": "Confirmed: `migrate` is CID/provider migration (blob pinning across IPFS providers), unrelated to IPNS sequence. My investigation is complete.\n\n# TEE IPNS Republisher: Map & Sequence Behavior\n\n## 1. Where is the republisher? What triggers it?\n\nTwo-part architecture: the **API orchestrates and enumerates**, the **TEE worker decrypts + signs**.\n\n- **TEE worker (signing enclave):** `apps/tee-worker/` — an Express service. Signing route: `apps/tee-worker/src/routes/republish.ts` (`POST /republish`). Actual signing: `apps/tee-worker/src/services/ipns-signer.ts`.\n- **API orchestrator:** `apps/api/src/republish/`. The cron trigger is a BullMQ repeatable job registered in `apps/api/src/republish/republish.module.ts:31-43`:\n  ```ts\n  await this.queue.upsertJobScheduler('republish-cron',\n    { pattern: '0 */6 * * *', // Every 6 hours: 00:00, 06:00, 12:00, 18:00\n    }, { name: 'republish-batch' });\n  ```\n  The BullMQ worker `apps/api/src/republish/republish.processor.ts:28` calls `republishService.processRepublishBatch()`. Interval is also coded as `REPUBLISH_INTERVAL_HOURS = 6` (`republish.service.ts:17`).\n\n## 2. Does it DECRYPT the IPNS private key and RE-SIGN?\n\nYes. In the TEE worker, `apps/tee-worker/src/routes/republish.ts:71-80`:\n```ts\nconst { ipnsPrivateKey: decryptedKey, usedEpoch } = await decryptWithFallback(\n  encryptedIpnsKey, entry.currentEpoch, entry.previousEpoch);\nipnsPrivateKey = decryptedKey;\n// 3. Sign IPNS record with incremented sequence number\nconst newSequenceNumber = BigInt(entry.sequenceNumber) + 1n;\nconst signedRecord = await signIpnsRecord(ipnsPrivateKey, entry.latestCid, newSequenceNumber);\n```\n`signIpnsRecord` (`ipns-signer.ts:30`) calls `createIpnsRecord(...)`, which produces a fully re-signed V1+V2 record via the `ipns` package (`packages/core/src/ipns/create-record.ts:67`, `ipnsCreate(libp2pPrivateKey, value, sequenceNumber, lifetimeMs, { v1Compatible: true })`). The key is zeroed immediately after (`republish.ts:93-94`).\n\n## 3. What sequence does it embed — keep or bump?\n\n**It always bumps by +1.** This is the load-bearing answer. `apps/tee-worker/src/routes/republish.ts:79`:\n```ts\nconst newSequenceNumber = BigInt(entry.sequenceNumber) + 1n;\n```\nThe bumped value is embedded directly into the re-signed record (`createIpnsRecord` takes `sequenceNumber` as an explicit param and CBOR-encodes it — `create-record.ts:31-36, 67`). The new sequence is returned as `newSequenceNumber` and the API persists it back to both the schedule and `FolderIpns`:\n- `republish.service.ts:139` `entry.sequenceNumber = result.newSequenceNumber;`\n- `republish.service.ts:158-163` → `syncFolderIpnsSequence(...)` updates `FolderIpns.sequenceNumber` + stored `signedRecord` (`republish.service.ts:379-385`).\n\nThere is no branch that preserves the existing sequence — every republish unconditionally embeds `existing + 1`.\n\n## 4. Does republish change the VALUE (CID)?\n\nNo CID change. It keeps the same value with refreshed validity. The value passed is `entry.latestCid` (`republish.ts:80`), which the API sourced from the schedule row's `latestCid` (`republish.service.ts:101`). What refreshes is the validity window: the TEE uses a 48-hour lifetime (`ipns-signer.ts:12`, `TEE_RECORD_LIFETIME_MS = 48h`) to cover the 6-hour interval. So republish = same CID, higher sequence, new 48h validity.\n\n## 5. Does it iterate ALL records or only flagged ones?\n\nOnly **due** entries, enumerated from the DB. `republish.service.ts:43-52` `getDueEntries()`:\n```ts\nwhere: { status: In(['active', 'retrying']),\n         nextRepublishAt: LessThanOrEqual(new Date()) },\norder: { nextRepublishAt: 'ASC' }, take: 2000,\n```\nSource table is `ipns_republish_schedule` (`republish-schedule.entity.ts`), populated via `enrollFolder()` (`republish.service.ts:211`) when a folder/file is published with a TEE-encrypted IPNS key. Entries are batched at `BATCH_SIZE = 100` and sent to the TEE (`republish.service.ts:93-109`). So it is per-enrolled-record, gated by `status` and `nextRepublishAt`, capped at 2000/run.\n\n## 6. Is republish a natural vehicle for migrating embedded-0 → embedded-1?\n\nYes, with caveats. Because every republish unconditionally re-signs with `BigInt(stored) + 1n` and embeds that into the record, the first republish of any enrolled record whose stored `sequenceNumber` is `0` will publish an embedded-`1` record (and persist `1` back to both `ipns_republish_schedule` and `FolderIpns`). The DB column defaults to `0` (`republish-schedule.entity.ts:59`), so a freshly-enrolled embedded-0 record naturally rolls to embedded-1 on its next 6-hour cycle — no separate migration step is needed for the embedded sequence itself.\n\nCaveats / dependencies on whether it actually fires:\n- The record must be **enrolled** in `ipns_republish_schedule` (TEE-encrypted key present). Records never enrolled are never touched by this path.\n- It must be `active`/`retrying` and `nextRepublishAt <= now` to be picked up.\n- The TEE must decrypt + sign successfully and the delegated-routing publish must succeed (`republish.service.ts:133-178`); on failure the entry isn't advanced (backoff, then `stale` after 10 failures, `republish.service.ts:347-352`), so embedded-0 would persist until a successful run.\n\nNote: the separate `apps/tee-worker/src/routes/migrate.ts` / `migration-worker.ts` is **CID/provider blob migration** (pinning across IPFS providers), not IPNS-sequence migration — it does not touch sequences.\n\n### Key files\n- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/tee-worker/src/routes/republish.ts` (sequence bump: line 79; signing: line 80)\n- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/tee-worker/src/services/ipns-signer.ts` (48h lifetime; delegates to core)\n- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/packages/core/src/ipns/create-record.ts` (embeds sequence into signed record)\n- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/api/src/republish/republish.service.ts` (enumeration, +1 persist, FolderIpns sync)\n- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/api/src/republish/republish.module.ts` (6h cron: `0 */6 * * *`)\n- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/api/src/republish/republish.processor.ts` (BullMQ worker entrypoint)\n- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/api/src/republish/republish-schedule.entity.ts` (`sequence_number` bigint default 0)"

> AGENT

Here's what it actually looks like — the recon found the migration is **far more tractable than "re-sign every live record via ops tooling,"** because the TEE republisher already does the work.

## Why a "migration" is needed at all

The embedded sequence is the `"Sequence"` key inside the **signed CBOR blob** (`crates/core/src/ipns.rs:142`), covered by the V2 signature (`compute_v2_signature`, `ipns.rs:220`). Verify reads the sequence *from that signed blob*, not the plaintext protobuf copy. So you **cannot change a live record's embedded sequence without re-signing** — which needs the Ed25519 IPNS private key.

And critically: **the API server cannot re-sign.** It stores only the ECIES-wrapped key (`folder_ipns.encrypted_ipns_private_key`) and has no signing primitive at all. Only the **client** (next publish) or the **TEE** (republish) can produce a new signed record.

## The population is small, scoped, and self-healing

Only the two FUSE interactive folder-create paths embed `0` — `mkdir.rs:173` and `windows/write_ops.rs:201`. The TS SDK and the FUSE replay path already embed `1`. So the live embedded-0 set = **FUSE/desktop-created folders that have never been re-published since creation.** You can't query `sequence_number = 0` (the API hard-codes first-publish to `'1'` and a backfill already normalized it) — you pre-filter `folder_ipns` rows at `sequence_number = '1'` then decode `signed_record`'s CBOR to confirm `embedded == 0`.

**The decisive finding (one reader was wrong, verified against source):** the TEE republish cron (`apps/tee-worker/src/routes/republish.ts:79`) does `newSequenceNumber = BigInt(entry.sequenceNumber) + 1n`, re-signs, and persists it. So **every enrolled embedded-0 record migrates itself to embedded-2 within one 6-hour cycle** (DB reports 2, embedded is 2 → strict equality holds). No bespoke migration tooling required for enrolled rows — natural rollover drains them.

## So the "migration" is really three small things

| What | Mechanism | Who | Risk |
|------|-----------|-----|------|
| Stop minting new embedded-0 records | Change `mkdir.rs:173` + `windows/write_ops.rs:201` to embed `1` | code change | low — hard prerequisite |
| Drain enrolled live embedded-0 records | Natural TEE rollover (1 cycle), optionally forced by setting `nextRepublishAt = now()` on the schedule rows | TEE | low, bounded, idempotent |
| Un-enrolled tail (no TEE key) | Only advances on the client's next folder mutation — user-driven, unbounded in time | client | this is the residual that gates "when can we flip" |

Plus one caveat the recon flagged: the **SDK vault-key blob first-publish** (`packages/sdk-core/src/vault/index.ts:44`) also embeds `0` — separate from the folder population, must be checked before flipping the SDK layer.

## What breaks if you flip strict equality too early

Fail-**closed**, hard error (not a warning). Every newly-created FUSE folder fails its own first resolve (create still embeds 0, API reports 1), and every un-migrated embedded-0 folder becomes unreadable via both the mount and the web/SDK — until re-published. That's the CR-01 regression. Recoverable, but a hard outage for affected records.

---

Given this — natural rollover drains enrolled records cheaply, but the un-enrolled tail + fresh-create window mean the flip genuinely can't be a standalone change — here's the now-informed sequencing question:

That's a smart instinct, and it could collapse the whole migration stream. If staging has no data worth keeping, there's no embedded-0 population to drain — and the strict flip becomes safe immediately after FUSE embed-1. The bigger question you're really asking is whether to **rip out every degraded-acceptance path** (the `None → legacy` no-signature tolerance *and* the embedded-0 skew allowance) so verify is strictly fail-closed on anything not fully V2-verified.

Before I recommend, two things decide whether that rip-out is clean or has landmines:
1. **Every producer must emit strict-compliant records** — if any code path still emits a V1-only/no-signature record or an embedded-0 first publish (the SDK vault-blob at `vault/index.ts:44` is one already), it fails-closed the moment legacy tolerance is gone.
2. **The data boundary** — wiping staging is fine, but removing legacy paths also affects **production**, which runs a real Phala TEE. If prod has real records, "nuke and rip out" isn't free there.

Let me map every degraded-acceptance path and audit every record producer to see if the full rip-out is actually clean.

So in practice \"allow + flag\" is just \"allow\" — the flag never gates anything downstream.\n\n## 2. Skew allowance — confirmed at :285-292\n\n```ts\nconst seqOk =\n  embeddedSeqBigInt === responseSeqBigInt ||\n  (responseSeqBigInt === 1n && embeddedSeqBigInt === 0n);\nif (!seqOk) {\n  throw new Error(\n    `IPNS sequence binding mismatch: embedded=${embeddedSeq}, response sequenceNumber=${response.sequenceNumber}`\n  );\n}\n```\n\nRationale comment at `:276-284`: first-publish exception mirroring the API's D-09 gate (`ipns.service.ts::upsertFolderIpns`). On a brand-new name the API stores DB `sequenceNumber=1` while accepting an embedded `0` (Rust/FUSE embed IPNS-native `0`) or `1` (TS SDK embeds `1`). Requires the producers in item 5/below to also be reconciled so first-publish embeds match DB.\n4. **Add EOL/expiry + V1 rejection** to match `verify-record.ts` strictness: either switch the resolve verification to call `verifyIpnsRecordSignature(ipnsName, marshalledRecord)` (which runs `ipns/validator` `validate()` — enforces V2-only, EOL, name binding), or add an explicit expiry check on the decoded CBOR `Validity`/`ValidityType`. The current raw-Ed25519 `verifyIpnsSignature` has no expiry/V1 guard.\n5. **`signatureVerified` becomes vacuously true** (function only returns on the verified path), so it can be dropped from the return type — but since the call would now throw on any unverified record, the strict invariant (\"valid V2 sig AND embedded==response seq AND CID binding\") is enforced by construction. The name↔key check (`:230-237`) and CID binding (`:249-259`) are already strict and stay as-is.\n\nBranches that must change, exhaustively: `:218` gate condition, `:293-295` legacy `else` (delete), `:285-287` skew disjunct (delete), plus add expiry/V1 enforcement (new). 17	        "label": "api-resolve-and-codec-tolerance",
18	        "text": "I have everything needed. Here's the analysis.\n\n# API Tolerance of Unverified / Partial / Legacy IPNS Records\n\n## 1. Publish without a V2 signature — verify is MANDATORY\n\n`publishRecord` rejects any record whose Ed25519 SignatureV2 does not verify against the key the IPNS name encodes. There is no bypass — `verifyIpnsRecordSignature` is called unconditionally for every publish. `apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts:87-89`:\n\n```ts\nif (!(await verifyIpnsRecordSignature(dto.ipnsName, recordBytes))) {\n  throw new BadRequestException('IPNS record signature verification failed');\n}\n```\n\nA record missing the V2 signature fields fails verification (verifier needs SignatureV2 to validate) and is rejected with a 400. The `publicKey`/`ipnsName` correspondence check (`:64-81`) is *optional* (gated on `dto.publicKey !== undefined`), but the signature chokepoint is not. So: publish is verify-mandatory; you cannot publish an unsigned/partial record.\n\n## 2. Resolve of legacy rows with `signed_record = NULL`\n\nresolveRecord does NOT verify on resolve — it only parses. For a legacy DB row with `signedRecord = NULL`, `parseCachedRecord` skips the parse branch entirely and returns a CID+sequence-only record with no signature fields. `apps/api/src/ipns/ipns-record.codec.ts:53-83`:\n\n```ts\nif (!cached?.latestCid) {\n  return null;\n}\nif (cached.signedRecord) {\n  // ... parse + attach signatureV2/data/pubKey\n}\nreturn { cid: cached.latestCid, sequenceNumber: cached.sequenceNumber };\n```\n\nSo a legacy row IS returned (HTTP 200), as long as `latestCid` is non-null — just without `signatureV2`/`data`/`pubKey`. The controller emits these as an all-or-nothing bundle, so a no-signature record returns only `success/cid/sequenceNumber`. `apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.controller.ts:203-213`:\n\n```ts\nconst hasSigData = result.signatureV2 && result.data && result.pubKey;\nreturn {\n  success: true,\n  cid: result.cid,\n  sequenceNumber: result.sequenceNumber,\n  ...(hasSigData && { signatureV2: result.signatureV2, data: result.data, pubKey: result.pubKey }),\n};\n```\n\nThe client receives an unverifiable record (no signature) and the API does not flag it — it just omits the signature fields. Resolve never returns 4xx for a missing-signature cached row; it 404s only when nothing resolves anywhere (`ipns.service.ts:540` / `controller.ts:194-196`).\n\n## 3. parseCachedRecord tolerates embedded-sequence != DB-sequence (skew)\n\nYes — explicitly. It parses the signed record bytes but then OVERRIDES both cid and sequenceNumber with the DB columns, treating DB as authoritative. `ipns-record.codec.ts:67-75`:\n\n```ts\n// Use the DB columns as authoritative — sequenceNumber is always\n// incremented by upsertFolderIpns, while the record bytes may contain\n// the client's pre-increment value (e.g. sequence 0 on first publish).\nif (parsed.cid !== cached.latestCid) {\n  logger.warn(`Cached signed record CID mismatch ...`);\n}\nreturn { ...parsed, cid: cached.latestCid, sequenceNumber: cached.sequenceNumber };\n```\n\nSo embedded sequence 0 with DB sequence 1 is tolerated silently (CID mismatch only warns; sequence skew isn't even logged). The returned `signatureV2`/`data`/`pubKey` come from the embedded (pre-increment, embedded-seq=0) bytes, while `sequenceNumber` reports the DB value (1) — meaning the bundle the client verifies carries the embedded seq, not the reported DB seq. This is the legacy \"first publish stores embedded-0\" skew being intentionally absorbed on the read path.\n\n## 4. Change sites for a strict / no-legacy regime\n\nTo enforce a strict (no-legacy, always-verifiable) regime, these are the candidate edit sites:\n\n- **Reject first-publish embedded sequence 0.** `ipns.service.ts:279-285` currently allows embedded ∈ {0n, 1n} on first publish. A strict regime drops `0n` (require embedded sequence to start at `1`), eliminating the skew at the source. Note `:356-357` already forces DB `sequenceNumber = '1'` on create regardless.\n- **Reject resolve of rows without `signed_record`.** `parseCachedRecord` (`ipns-record.codec.ts:61-82`) — change the fallback `return { cid, sequenceNumber }` (lines 81-82) to return `null` (→ 404) when `signedRecord` is NULL, so unverifiable legacy rows are never served.\n- **Reject the embedded≠DB skew instead of overriding.** `ipns-record.codec.ts:70-75` — instead of overriding `sequenceNumber` with the DB value and warning on CID mismatch, treat a mismatch as a hard error / discard. (Would also require embedded-1 first-publish per the bullet above so the parsed seq actually matches the DB seq.)\n- **Make the embedded-vs-DTO CID gate strict at publish.** Already present and strict at `ipns.service.ts:268-273` (full-value equality). No change needed, but it's the publish-side anchor that a strict regime relies on.\n- **Backfill / reject NULL `public_key` on resolve.** `withCachedPublicKey` (`ipns-record.codec.ts:85-97`) silently no-ops when `publicKey` is NULL, leaving the bundle unverifiable (no `pubKey`). A strict regime would require `public_key` to be non-null for the all-or-nothing bundle to ever be emitted.\n- **Controller bundle emission.** `ipns.controller.ts:198-213` — the `hasSigData` all-or-nothing gate is where you'd additionally 404/410 (rather than 200) when a record lacks a verifiable bundle, if you want strictness surfaced to the client rather than silently degraded.\n\n## 5. Does anything distinguish a 'legacy' (pre-#448, no-signature) record from a current one?\n\nThere is **no explicit discriminator field** (no `version`, no `isLegacy`, no schema-version column). Legacy status is **inferred structurally** from nullable columns on `FolderIpns`:\n\n- `signed_record` is `nullable: true` — `entities/folder-ipns.entity.ts:56-57`. A legacy/pre-signature row has `signedRecord = NULL`; a current row has it populated. This is the de-facto legacy marker.\n- `public_key` is `nullable: true` — `entities/folder-ipns.entity.ts:63-64`. Pre-#448 rows that never stored the raw key are NULL; resolve cannot complete signature verification for them (no `pubKey` in the bundle).\n- `latest_cid` nullable (`:42-43`), `encrypted_ipns_private_key`/`key_epoch` nullable (`:71,79`) — these distinguish \"never published\" / \"not TEE-enrolled\" rather than legacy-signature status specifically.\n\nSo the API does store a *structural* legacy signal (`signedRecord IS NULL` and/or `publicKey IS NULL`) but does not return or surface it to clients — resolve simply omits the signature bundle (controller `hasSigData` is false), and metrics tag the resolve `source: 'db_cache'` (`ipns.controller.ts:199`), which is the closest observable proxy for \"served an unverifiable/legacy record.\"\n\n### Relevant files\n- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts`\n- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipns/ipns-record.codec.ts`\n- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.controller.ts`\n- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipns/entities/folder-ipns.entity.ts`"

So any row written before the `17432`/`17433` migrations ran has `signed_record = NULL` and `public_key = NULL` — i.e. **legacy pre-signature records are structurally possible** in a long-lived DB.\n- `1740500000000-BackfillIpnsSequenceNumbers.ts` is unrelated to signatures — it only bumps `sequence_number` `0`→`1` for non-root rows with content (an off-by-one fix). It does NOT touch signature fields and is non-reversible (`down()` is a no-op, `:33-37`).\n- The resolve path actively tolerates these NULLs today (the \"legacy paths\"): `ipns.service.ts:494` guards `cached?.publicKey` before enriching, `:226` guards `existing?.signedRecord` for anti-rollback, and `:512-520` enriches a network result with cached signature fields only `if (...!result.signatureV2 && cachedResult.signatureV2)`. Removing those branches is what \"removing legacy IPNS paths\" means.\n\n### 2. DB reset/wipe is a documented, supported operation; staging is explicitly disposable\n\n- `docs/DATABASE_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md:260-266` documents a full reset: `dropdb cipherbox && createdb cipherbox`, then restart the API — `migrationsRun: true` recreates all tables from migrations on an empty DB.\n- Environment matrix (`DATABASE_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md:242-248`) treats Staging and Production as distinct; both run migrations via `run-migrations.ts` in Docker. Deploy executes `node dist/run-migrations.js` before app start (`:272-279`).\n- **Staging data is explicitly disposable, no backups:** `.planning/milestones/m1/phases/09.1-env-devops-staging/09.1-CONTEXT.md:21` \"Staging data is disposable — no backups needed\"; reiterated `09.1-RESEARCH.md:590`.\n- The product surfaces this to testers: `.planning/quick/004-add-staging-environment-banner/004-PLAN.md:87` shows the banner text `STAGING | Testing only — data may be wiped without notice`.\n- Precedent: the team has done full DB wipes before as a deliberate \"clean break\" (Phase 11.2 / 12.6): `.planning/milestones/m2/phases/11.2-remove-v1-folder-metadata/GOAL.md:11` \"we'll nuke the DB\"; `12.6-CONTEXT.md:25` \"Vault wipe window means no legacy data exists; only v2 schema implemented\"; `12.6-05-SUMMARY.md:103` \"DB wipe can proceed safely.\"\n\n### 3. Self-bootstrap on empty DB — a wipe is NON-destructive to a returning user who still holds their keys\n\nThis is the load-bearing finding. **The DB holds only derivable/republishable state; the source of truth is the user's Web3Auth-derived private key.**\n\n- All vault IPNS keys are **deterministically derived from the user's `privateKey`**, not stored server-side:\n  - `packages/core/src/vault/init.ts:42` `deriveVaultIpnsKeypair(userPrivateKey)`; the doc comment `:60` says \"The IPNS public key is NOT stored -- it is derivable from the private key.\"\n- The login flow keys off `existingVault` (the API `vaults` row). After a wipe, that row is gone, so login takes the **new-user branch** and republishes everything fresh, strict-compliant:\n  - `apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts:172-231` — `else { initializeVault(...) }` re-derives the root IPNS keypair (`:176`), re-derives the separate vault-key IPNS keypair (`:179`), re-wraps `rootFolderKey` and re-publishes the v2 key blob (`:183-196`), re-publishes empty root folder metadata (`:200-213`), and re-registers the vault via `vaultApi.initVault` (`:216`).\n  - Because the IPNS name is derived from the same private key, the user gets the **same IPNS name** back. New publishes carry `signed_record` + `public_key` (strict-compliant), so post-wipe there are zero legacy-NULL rows.\n- Caveat — **actual file content is NOT re-created by login.** Login only re-publishes an EMPTY root folder (`useAuth.ts:200` `children: []`). The returning user's keys are recovered, but their previously-uploaded files/folder tree are NOT restored by the bootstrap — recovery of prior content depends on the old IPNS records / pinned CIDs still being resolvable on the network, which a DB wipe does not by itself re-pin. For staging (disposable, testing-only) this is acceptable; the relevant point is the user does not \"lose their identity/vault\" and re-login works cleanly.\n- Republish schedule self-heals: every publish auto-enrolls into `ipns_republish_schedule` (`ipns.service.ts:333-348`, `:375-388`), so a wiped schedule table is repopulated on next publish.\n\n### 4. Production data sensitivity — KNOWN vs MUST-CONFIRM\n\n- KNOWN: There is **no production deploy workflow** in the repo — only `deploy-staging.yml`, `tag-staging.yml`, `desktop-staging-release.yml`, `deploy-landing.yml` (`.github/workflows/`). Staging is on Sapphire **Devnet**, production would be Sapphire **Mainnet** (`ENVIRONMENTS.md:39`, matrix `:18`), and production Web3Auth setup is explicitly **deferred** (\"set up when ready to go live\", `09.1-CONTEXT.md:68`). This strongly implies **production is not deployed and has no real users** as of these docs.\n- MUST-CONFIRM by operator: There is **no doc that explicitly asserts \"production has zero real users.\"** The safety of the wipe is established only for **staging** (explicitly disposable). Before wiping anything, the operator must confirm the target is the staging DB (`ssh root@76.13.151.200`, per memory) and NOT a production DB, and confirm no production instance with real user data exists.\n\n### 5. What \"nuke staging DB\" touches — IPNS/vault-related tables\n\nAll created by `1700000000000-FullSchema.ts`. The IPNS/vault state lives in:\n\n- `vaults` (`FullSchema.ts:95-106`) — the vault-existence record (`owner_id`, `owner_public_key`, `root_ipns_name`). Login's `existingVault` check reads this; wiping it forces the fresh-bootstrap branch.\n- `folder_ipns` (`:141-158`) — IPNS-name→CID cache + `signed_record`/`public_key`/`encrypted_ipns_private_key`/`key_epoch`. Holds the legacy-NULL rows.\n- `ipns_republish_schedule` (`:199-220`) — TEE republish enrollment; self-heals on next publish.\n- `pinned_cids` (`:121-133`) — CID pin/quota tracking.\n- `users` (`:30-38`) + `auth_methods` (`:67-80`) + `refresh_tokens` (`:44-56`) — identity; FK `ON DELETE CASCADE` means deleting a user cascades to `vaults`, `folder_ipns`, `pinned_cids`, `ipns_republish_schedule` (all FK to `users.id`).\n- `tee_key_state` / `tee_key_rotation_log` (`:166-194`) — TEE epoch state (singleton + audit). A wipe drops the current epoch; it is re-seeded by the TEE worker/bootstrap, but worth flagging to the operator.\n\n### Bottom line\n\n- KNOWN safe-to-wipe: staging is explicitly disposable with a user-facing \"data may be wiped\" banner, full DB reset is a documented supported operation, the team has done clean-break wipes before, and a returning user's vault **identity/keys self-bootstrap fresh and strict-compliant on next login** (keys are derived, not stored). Removing the legacy NULL-tolerance paths in `ipns.service.ts` is safe IF the DB no longer contains legacy NULL rows — a wipe guarantees that.\n- KNOWN caveat: a wipe does not restore previously-uploaded file *content* (login republishes an empty root); fine for staging.\n- MUST CONFIRM with operator before acting: (a) the target is the staging DB, not production; (b) no production deployment with real user data exists (no production deploy workflow in-repo strongly suggests none, but no doc states it outright); (c) acceptance that `tee_key_state` is dropped and re-seeded."

> AGENT

The recon came back with a clear verdict and one important correction to your proposal. Bottom line: **yes, the strict rip-out is the right call and it's genuinely *simpler* than the gated migration** — with no prod and a wipeable staging that self-bootstraps strict-compliant records on next login, there's no population to migrate, no drain pass, no skew window. But the scope is meaningfully bigger than "fix 2-3 producer sites," and there are two holes the todo never mentioned.

## The catch: 7 producer sites, not 3

A strict `embedded == response` verify fails-closed on *any* first-publish record that embeds `0`. Beyond the two FUSE `mkdir` sites + the SDK vault-blob the todo names, the audit found **5 more** embed-0 first-publish producers:

| Site | Fires on |
|------|----------|
| `apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts:191` | web vault-key blob — **every new vault** |
| `apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts:208` | web **root folder** metadata — **every new vault** |
| `apps/web/src/services/vault-settings.service.ts:131` | web vault settings first publish |
| `crates/fuse/src/metadata.rs:557` | FUSE bin (`make_bin_record(0)`) first publish |
| `packages/sdk-core/src/vault/index.ts:44` | SDK vault-key blob (the named one) |

The two `useAuth.ts` ones are the killer — they're the *first records every fresh web user resolves*. Flipping strict verify without fixing them would fail-close every new login. So the cutover is **7 producer sites → embed 1** (+ tighten the API first-publish gate at `ipns.service.ts:279-285` to reject embedded 0).

The legacy/no-signature removal, by contrast, **is** clean: no producer anywhere emits a V1-only or unsigned record — legacy is purely a resolve-side tolerance artifact. So removing it can't break a producer.

## The full degraded-path inventory (14 branches, 4 layers)

Rust (`api-client` None→legacy, `verify.rs` Legacy variant + 9 caller arms, skew), TS (`sdk-core` legacy-else + skew), API (codec NULL-tolerance + skew-override, service legacy-enrich), and the cross-language vector (`legacy-absent`→invalid, `first-publish-skew`→invalid). All mapped to exact file:lines — that becomes the plan's task list.

## Two things the todo never accounted for

1. **SDK unverified bypasses** — `crates/sdk/src/registry.rs:170` and `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs:201` call raw `resolve_ipns` with **zero** verification (accepts a tampered CID — strictly worse than the legacy path). These are *different files* from the desktop `prepopulate.rs`/`vault.rs` the todo names, so there are more unverified resolve sites than captured. Closing them needs a verified wrapper in `api-client` (the chokepoint currently lives in `cipherbox-fuse`, which the SDK doesn't depend on).
2. **No resolve-side expiry check** — both verifiers check signature + binding but *not* record EOL on resolve, so a validly-signed expired record passes.

Three decisions shape the plan:

  "content": "# Phase 60: IPNS Verification Cross-Layer Closeout — Context\n\n**Gathered:** 2026-06-24\n**Status:** Ready for planning\n**Source:** Interactive discussion + two recon workflows (migration mechanics + degraded-path/producer audit)\n**Requirement:** HARD-11\n\n<domain>\n\n## Phase Boundary\n\nPhase 60 converts IPNS resolution to a **strict, fail-closed, zero-knowledge integrity model** across every layer, and recovers per-operation verification CPU on the API hot path. This makes a clean strict cutover strictly simpler than a gated record migration — there is no live population to migrate, no drain pass, and no skew window to maintain.\n\n</domain>\n\n<decisions>\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n### Approach\n\n- **D-01 — Strict / no-legacy rip-out, not a gated migration.** Do the full cutover: remove all degraded-acceptance branches, unify producers, regenerate the vector, and **wipe staging**. Do NOT build record-migration tooling, a TEE-drain pass, or a forward-compat skew window — the wipe + self-bootstrap removes the population those would serve. Local dev DBs must also be wiped (existing embedded-0 records would otherwise fail-closed until republished).\n- **D-12 — Land the cutover cross-layer in lockstep.** The Rust verify, TS resolve, API service/codec, and the cross-language vector strict changes must ship together; the embed-1 producer changes must ship together with strict verify and the staging wipe so no embedded-0 record exists when strict verify goes live. This is the CR-01 lesson (never flip one layer or flip before the source is unified).\n\n### Stream A — Strict verify cutover\n\n- **D-02 — Unify ALL first-publish producers to embed sequence `1`.** Seven sites (the two named in the todo plus five the recon found):\n  1. `crates/fuse/src/write_ops/implementation/mkdir.rs:173` (FUSE new folder)\n  2. `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:201` (Windows new folder)\n  3. `crates/fuse/src/metadata.rs:557` (FUSE bin first publish — `make_bin_record(0)`)\n  4. `packages/sdk-core/src/vault/index.ts:44` (SDK vault-key blob)\n  5. `apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts:191` (web vault-key blob — fires on every new vault)\n  6. `apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts:208` (web root-folder metadata — fires on every new vault)\n  7. `apps/web/src/services/vault-settings.service.ts:131` (web vault settings first publish)\n- **D-03 — Tighten the API first-publish gate to reject embedded `0`.** `apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts:279-285` currently accepts embedded ∈ {0n, 1n} on first publish; require it to start at `1` so the source of truth matches the unified producers. (`:356-357` already forces DB sequence `1`.)\n- **D-04 — Remove all Rust degraded-acceptance paths.** Delete the all-fields-absent `Ok(None)` legacy branch (`crates/api-client/src/ipns.rs:77-80`); remove the `VerifyError::Legacy` variant (`crates/fuse/src/verify.rs:21-24`, Display `:34-37`) and fold all 9 caller arms (`events.rs`, `metadata.rs` ×3, `publish.rs` ×2, `fs.rs`, `replay.rs` ×2) into their existing fail-closed `Invalid` handling; drop the skew disjunct at `verify.rs:124` to strict `embedded_seq == resp_seq`.\n- **D-05 — Remove all TS degraded-acceptance paths.** Delete the legacy `else` fall-through (`packages/sdk-core/src/ipns/index.ts:293-295`) so a record lacking signature fields throws; drop the skew disjunct (`:285-292`) to strict equality.\n- **D-06 — Remove all API degraded-acceptance paths.** `parseCachedRecord` returns `null` (→404) when `signed_record IS NULL` (`apps/api/src/ipns/ipns-record.codec.ts:81-82`); stop silently overriding embedded≠DB sequence skew (`:67-75`); remove the nullable-publicKey / cached-signature legacy-enrich branches (`ipns.service.ts:226`, `:494`, `:512-520`). Publish-side verify (`ipns.service.ts:87-89`) is already mandatory/strict and is the anchor — keep it.\n- **D-07 — Add resolve-side EOL/expiry enforcement.** Both verifiers currently check signature + binding but not record expiry on resolve; a validly-signed expired record passes today. Add expiry checks to the Rust verifier and route the TS resolve path through the validator (or add an explicit CBOR `Validity` check) so expired records fail-closed.\n- **D-10 — Regenerate the cross-language verify vector and tests.** In `scripts/gen-ipns-verify-vectors.ts` reclassify `legacy-absent` → `invalid` and `first-publish-skew` → `invalid` (in the generator, not by hand); regenerate `tests/vectors/ipns/verify.json` via `npx tsx`; update the `crates/fuse/tests/ipns_verify_vectors.rs` classifier (`:88-89`, `:134`) and the affected Rust unit tests (`verify.rs:244-253`, `:268-286`; `api-client/src/ipns.rs:233-238`).\n\n### Stream B — Verified-resolve coverage\n\n- **D-08 — Close the `crates/sdk` unverified bypasses via an `api-client` verified-resolve wrapper.** `crates/sdk/src/registry.rs:170` and `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs:201` call raw `resolve_ipns` with zero verification (accept tampered CIDs — broader than the legacy hole). The verified chokepoint currently lives in `cipherbox-fuse`, which the SDK does not depend on; add a verified-resolve wrapper in `crates/api-client` (or another crate the SDK + FUSE + desktop all depend on) and route these sites through it.\n- **D-09 — Route the desktop Tauri `resolve_ipns` sites through the verified resolver.** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/prepopulate.rs` (~43/110/177/236) and `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/vault.rs` (~21/250), with the same per-operation scoped fail-closed posture the FUSE sites use. Verify line numbers by symbol before editing.\n\n### Stream C — API hot-path verify caching\n\n- **D-11 — Recover per-op verification CPU on the publish/resolve hot path.** Implement a safe short-circuit (skip re-verifying DB-authoritative records this server just signed/persisted) and/or a short-TTL verified-record cache keyed by `(ipnsName, sequenceNumber, signature)`. MUST still fully verify externally-sourced / DHT records (someguy resolves) and anything not produced/persisted by this server — no integrity reduction for untrusted inputs. Deliver a measured per-op cost recovery (benchmark/prototype), per `docs/CAPACITY.md` §1.5.\n\n### Claude's Discretion\n\n- Exact crate placement of the shared verified-resolve wrapper (D-08) — `api-client` vs a new shared crate — chosen to minimize dependency churn while letting FUSE, SDK, and desktop all consume it.\n- Caching mechanism for D-11 (in-process map vs Redis short-TTL) and the exact short-circuit predicate, provided untrusted/DHT records are always verified.\n- Whether to express the lockstep changes as one wave or several, provided D-12's \"no embedded-0 record alive when strict verify ships\" invariant holds.\n\n</decisions>\n\n<canonical_refs>\n\n## Canonical References\n\n**Downstream agents MUST read these before planning or implementing.**\n\n### Phase scope todos (the three folded streams)\n\n- `.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-23-phase60-ipns-first-publish-strict-equality-cutover.md` — Stream A origin (strict-equality cutover, CR-01 history)\n- `.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-22-desktop-resolve-ipns-verified-coverage.md` — Stream B (desktop verified-resolve)\n- `.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-23-cache-redundant-ipns-signature-verification-hot-path.md` — Stream C (hot-path caching)\n\n### IPNS verification source of truth\n\n- `crates/fuse/src/verify.rs` — the verify chokepoint, the skew allowance (`:124`), and the Phase-60 deferral note (`:100-130`)\n- `crates/api-client/src/ipns.rs` — `verify_ipns_resolve_signature` (`Option<bool>` legacy contract, `:73-80`)\n- `crates/core/src/ipns.rs` — record build/sign (`build_cbor_data`, `create_ipns_record`); ## RESEARCH COMPLETE

**Phase:** 60 - IPNS Verification Cross-Layer Closeout
**Confidence:** HIGH

### Key Findings

**Inventory corrections (7 items):**
- `api-client/src/ipns.rs:77-80` → actual `Ok(None)` is at lines 78-79
- `verify.rs:68-72` `Legacy` arm → lines 69-71
- `parseCachedRecord` at `codec.ts:81-82` does NOT currently return null — it returns a cid-only struct. The strict change is adding `if (!cached.signedRecord) return null`
- CONTEXT items 11/13 line references: `withCachedPublicKey` is the enrich at :494; equal-seq enrich is at :512-519; no standalone enrich at `:226`
- TS legacy fall-through is at line 294, not 293-295 (console.warn is line 294; the else block spans 293-295)
- **2 missed producers**: `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/vault.rs:109` and `:154` both call `create_ipns_record(..., 0, ...)` for vault key blob and root folder init. Total first-publish producers = 9, not 7
- Desktop files are at `src/fuse/prepopulate.rs` and `src/commands/vault.rs`, not `src/prepopulate.rs` and `src/vault.rs`

**HOW question answers:**
1. **D-08 wrapper placement**: Move `resolve_ipns_verified` + `VerifyError` + `VerifiedResolve` + `bind_verified` from `crates/fuse/src/verify.rs` into `crates/api-client/src/ipns.rs`. Add `cipherbox-core` dep to `api-client`. No cycles. All consumers (sdk, fuse, desktop) already depend on api-client.
2. **D-07 EOL expiry**: `ipns/validator` `validate()` ALREADY checks EOL (confirmed in source). For TS: parse `Validity` bytes from the CBOR `data` field (already decoded for CID/seq binding) and compare to `Date.now()`. For Rust: extend `decode_ipns_cbor_data` to return validity bytes and add expiry check in `bind_verified`.
3. **D-11 caching**: The real hot-path cost is on PUBLISH (verifyIpnsRecordSignature called every publish). DB resolve paths are already short-circuited (no re-verify). Safe optimization: skip `verifyIpnsRecordSignature` on the TEE idempotent-republish path (internal only, gated on `isIdempotentRepublish === true`). In-process `Map<ipnsName+seq, timestamp>` with 60s TTL for any other caching. DHT records MUST always verify.
4. **D-12 wave sequencing**: Two waves — Wave 1: all code changes (9 producers embed-1, strict verify active, API gate tightened, codec/service degraded paths removed, desktop routed through verified wrapper, EOL added, vector regen); Wave 2: staging wipe + redeploy + smoke test. Must be atomic: strict verify active only after wipe completes.
5. **Blast radius**: `resolveIpnsRecord` will throw (not return `signatureVerified: false`) after Phase 60. Callers relying on the false return need updating. Pattern to grep: `resolveIpnsRecord` calls + `signatureVerified` references in `apps/web/src/` and `packages/sdk-core/src/`.

1. **Verified-resolve wrapper (`bind_verified` / `resolve_ipns_verified`) moves wholesale from `crates/fuse/src/verify.rs` into `crates/api-client/src/ipns.rs`** — the entire file is the analog; Phase 60 changes are surgical removals of the `Legacy` variant, the skew disjunct on line 124, and the `Ok(None)` branch on line 78-79 of `verify_ipns_resolve_signature`.

2. **Embed-1 producer pattern: `crates/fuse/src/replay.rs:628` is the canonical `create_ipns_record(..., 1, 86_400_000)` call** that all 9 sites must match — the change is one argument digit per site.

3. **All `VerifyError::Legacy` caller arms (9 across FUSE) collapse to `VerifyError::Invalid` handling** — the compiler enforces exhaustiveness and will surface all 9 sites as compile errors once the variant is deleted.

4. **TS legacy-else removal turns `resolveIpnsRecord` from warn+return into throw** — requires blast-radius audit of `signatureVerified` consumers before Wave 1 merge.

5. **EOL expiry: TS uses CBOR `Validity` bytes already in the decoded `cborFields` map (inline check); Rust extends `decode_ipns_cbor_data` return value then checks in `bind_verified`** — both use RFC3339 string comparison against `Date.now()` / `SystemTime::now()` with a 5-minute clock skew buffer.

6. **API strict anchor is `ipns.service.ts:87-89` (`verifyIpnsRecordSignature` mandatory throw)** — all other API tightening (codec null-return, gate drop of 0n, enrich removal) follows the same fail-closed posture already established there.

18:**Strategic context (drives the approach):** There is **no production instance** and staging data is explicitly disposable. The team will **wipe staging** as part of the cutover. Vault identity self-bootstraps on next login (all IPNS keys are deterministically derived from the Web3Auth private key, not stored server-side), so a wipe produces zero legacy / embedded-0 records and is non-destructive to a returning key-holder's identity. This makes a clean strict cutover strictly simpler than a gated record migration — there is no live population to migrate, no drain pass, and no skew window to maintain.
28:- **D-01 — Strict / no-legacy rip-out, not a gated migration.** Do the full cutover: remove all degraded-acceptance branches, unify producers, regenerate the vector, and **wipe staging**. Do NOT build record-migration tooling, a TEE-drain pass, or a forward-compat skew window — the wipe + self-bootstrap removes the population those would serve. Local dev DBs must also be wiped (existing embedded-0 records would otherwise fail-closed until republished).
86:- `apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts`, `ipns-record.codec.ts`, `ipns.controller.ts`, `entities/folder-ipns.entity.ts` — API resolve/publish/codec + the nullable `signed_record`/`public_key` legacy markers
128:- The embedded sequence is inside the signature-covered CBOR; changing it requires re-signing (Ed25519 key). The API server holds no plaintext key and cannot re-sign — but with the wipe + self-bootstrap, no re-sign migration is needed.

**Phase 60: IPNS Verification Cross-Layer Closeout** — 8 plans in 4 waves. Plan-checker: **VERIFICATION PASSED** (12 dimensions, 2 minor verify-command warnings applied).

| Wave | Plans | What it builds |
| ---- | ----- | -------------- |
| 1 | 60-01, 60-02, 60-03 | Relocate verified-resolve wrapper → `api-client` + strict Rust verify + Rust EOL (01); unify all **9** producers to embed `1` (02); strict TS resolve throw-path + EOL + consumer blast-radius audit (03) |
| 2 | 60-04, 60-05 | Fold 9 FUSE `Legacy` arms + route sdk/desktop resolve sites through the wrapper + delete `verify.rs` (04); API strict gate + codec null→404 + remove legacy enrich (05) |
| 3 | 60-06, 60-07 | Hot-path verify-cost benchmark + safe short-TTL cache, DHT records always verified (06); regenerate cross-language vector + strict classifier (07) |
| 4 | 60-08 | **Manual** lockstep cutover: cross-layer gate confirmation + staging wipe + smoke (`autonomous: false`) |

**How your decisions landed:**
- **Strict rip-out** — all 14 degraded-acceptance branches removed across Rust/TS/API; legacy/None + skew + NULL-tolerance gone, fail-closed everywhere.
- **SDK bypasses closed** — wrapper moves to `crates/api-client` (D-08) so FUSE, sdk (`registry.rs`/`sync.rs`), and desktop all consume one chokepoint.
- **Expiry included** — EOL checks on both Rust and TS resolve paths.
- **Lockstep safe** — producers embed `1` in Wave 1 before strict verify activates; D-08 wrapper precedes routing; staging wipe is the final manual step.
- The planner also caught a research error: the TEE republish path bypasses `verifyIpnsRecordSignature` entirely, so it **prohibited** the suggested TEE skip — the cache only short-circuits exact-bytes client re-submission, never DHT/untrusted records.

**On the branch:** the planner committed cleanly on the feat branch — no stray `docs/plan-phase-60` branch was created, so no cleanup was needed.
